````math
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}(\Gamma)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\mathcal{L}(S_{v}\mid U_{v},C)=\mathcal{L}(S_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C)\quad P\text{-a.s.}}
``` |  | (21) |

Thus this appendix introduces no additional objective: it is an exact
reformulation of the same local predictive deficiency used by the
recursive closure result. The finite-test and empirical restrictions
begin only in
Appendix [F.3](#A6.SS3 "F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

### F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates

This subsection supplies the approximation and calibration chain used in
Section [3](#S3 "3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and the projection derivation used in
Section [3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 Margin-Relaxed Predictive Constraints ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
In particular,
Appendix [F.3.5](#A6.SS3.SSS5 "F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
proves the dual characterization stated in
Proposition [2](#Thmprop2 "Proposition 2 (Dual Characterization of Predictive Feasibility Projection). ‣ 3.2 Margin-Relaxed Predictive Constraints ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

This appendix connects the ideal local predictive deficiency in the main
text to its computable training statistic. The purpose is deliberately
limited. We do not introduce a second root-level theorem, nor do we
identify the empirical statistic with conditional mutual information.
Instead, we isolate the successive restrictions used by the
implementation and make their residuals explicit:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}\;\longrightarrow\;\Delta_{v}^{\rm test}\;\longrightarrow\;\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}\;\longrightarrow\;\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}.}
``` |  | (22) |

The main full-covariance route has three residuals:
$`\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}`$ for finite-test coverage,
$`\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}`$ for the readout class, and
$`\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v}`$ for finite-sample estimation. The
optional diagonal-covariance variant introduces $`D_{v}^{\rm diag}`$
between the profiled value and its empirical diagonal estimate, with an
additional $`\varepsilon_{{\rm diag},v}`$ residual.

Throughout, let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S_{v}:=(Y_{v},Y_{\operatorname{pa}(v)}),\qquad Z_{v}^{\Gamma}:=\Gamma(U_{v}),\qquad C:=(C_{w})_{w\in V},
``` |  |

and use the main-text local predictive deficiency

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}(\Gamma):=I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C).}
``` |  | (23) |

If an interface is usable only when both events of the adjacent
predictive pair are present, let $`M_{v}`$ be its validity indicator and
write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{v}[F]:=\frac{\mathbb{E}[M_{v}F]}{\mathbb{E}[M_{v}]},\qquad\mathbb{P}(M_{v}=1)>0.
``` |  |

The calibration statements below are evaluated under this same
eligible-interface law. For Hilbert-valued $`f`$, write
$`\|f\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}\|f\|^{2}`$. Censored
observations are omitted rather than encoded as zero-valued targets.
When every theoretical interface is observed, this law is the main-text
law. With censoring, identifying the eligible-interface local predictive
deficiency with the unconditional main-text local predictive deficiency
requires the corresponding support/selection condition; without that
condition, the bounds below calibrate the local predictive deficiency on
the observed eligible interfaces only.

##### Scope.

The empirical statistic is not claimed to be an exact finite-sample
estimator of the local predictive deficiency. For a fixed interface law,
the exact local criterion is reduced through finite predictive tests, a
restricted readout class, and full-covariance empirical moments. We
bound their residual terms under the stated regularity conditions. The
root-level information-theoretic guarantee in the main text applies to
the ideal local predictive deficiencies. The calibration results below
quantify the corresponding local approximation; the pooled training
statistic used by
Algorithm [2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.3 Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is treated separately when its interface-wise differential is
constructed.

#### F.3.1 Exact Local Criterion and Context Localization

The main-text criterion in
Equation [23](#A6.E23 "In F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
conditions on the retained global context $`C`$, whereas the implemented
statistic uses the context $`C_{v}`$ visible at interface $`v`$. Let
$`C_{-v}:=(C_{w})_{w\in V\setminus\{v\}}`$. We use the following
localization condition only to identify these two conditioning choices:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S_{v}\perp C_{-v}\mid(U_{v},C_{v}),\qquad S_{v}\perp C_{-v}\mid(Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}).
``` |  | (24) |

By the chain rule,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v},C_{-v})={}`$ | $`\displaystyle I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+I(S_{v};C_{-v}\mid U_{v},Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v})-I(S_{v};C_{-v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}).`$ |  |

The last two terms vanish under
Equation [24](#A6.E24 "In F.3.1 Exact Local Criterion and Context Localization ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
using that $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ is measurable with respect to $`U_{v}`$.
Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}(\Gamma)=I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}).}
``` |  | (25) |

Without
Equation [24](#A6.E24 "In F.3.1 Exact Local Criterion and Context Localization ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
the construction below should be read as a local-context surrogate. The
pre-compression reference is $`U_{v}`$ throughout this appendix.

For the comparison with the main-text defect below, take $`M_{v}=1`$
almost surely. With censored interfaces, the same derivations apply to
the defect and conditional laws under the eligible-interface law;
identifying that defect with the unconditional one needs an additional
selection assumption. Under full observation, the variational identity
remains exact after localization:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\delta_{v}(\Gamma)=\inf_{q_{v}}\mathbb{E}_{v}\!\left[D_{\rm KL}\!\left(P_{S_{v}\mid U_{v},C_{v}}\,\|\,q_{v}(\cdot\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v})\right)\right].
``` |  | (26) |

Every step below is a restriction or approximation of this local
predictive problem, not a replacement for
Equation [26](#A6.E26 "In F.3.1 Exact Local Criterion and Context Localization ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

#### F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests

Following
Section [3](#S3 "3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
P_{v}:=T_{S}\!\left(\chi(C_{v})\otimes\phi(Y_{v},Y_{\operatorname{pa}(v)})\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{P}},
``` |  |

where $`\chi`$, $`\phi`$, and $`T_{S}`$ are fixed. The test vector
supplies supervision only; it is not an additional input to the
recursive host. Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\overline{P}_{v}:=P_{v}-\mathbb{E}_{v}[P_{v}\mid C_{v}],\qquad m_{X,v}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\overline{P}_{v}\mid X_{v},C_{v}],\quad X_{v}\in\{U_{v},Z_{v}^{\Gamma}\}.
``` |  |

Because the $`C_{v}`$-measurable baseline can be absorbed into the
predictor, predicting $`P_{v}`$ and predicting $`\overline{P}_{v}`$ have
the same Bayes-risk difference. Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(P_{v}\mid X_{v},C_{v}):=\inf_{g}\mathbb{E}_{v}\|P_{v}-g(X_{v},C_{v})\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  |

Conditional expectation is the $`L^{2}`$ projection onto
$`\sigma(X_{v},C_{v})`$. Since $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}=\Gamma(U_{v})`$, the
tower property gives
$`m_{Z,v}=\mathbb{E}_{v}[m_{U,v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}]`$, and
therefore

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{v}^{\rm test}(\Gamma)&:=\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(P_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v})-\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(P_{v}\mid U_{v},C_{v})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{v}\|m_{U,v}-m_{Z,v}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0.\end{aligned}}
``` |  | (27) |

This is exactly the finite-test value gap used in the main text. The
identity is exact for the selected tests; the approximation is the use
of those tests in place of the full conditional law of $`S_{v}`$.

To make this approximation explicit, let $`\psi_{v}(S_{v},C_{v})`$ take
values in a separable Hilbert test space $`\mathcal{G}_{v}^{\rm test}`$.
Let $`\Pi_{m,v}`$ be the orthogonal projector onto the finite test span
used by $`P_{v}`$, and let $`Q_{m,v}`$ be an isometric coordinate map on
that span. Define the full conditional predictive change

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
e_{v}(\Gamma):=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\psi_{v}(S_{v},C_{v})\mid U_{v},C_{v}]-\mathbb{E}_{v}[\psi_{v}(S_{v},C_{v})\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}].
``` |  |

We use the following quantitative coverage condition on the admissible
law and compressor class: for some finite $`c_{v}^{(0)}>0`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\delta_{v}(\Gamma)\leq c_{v}^{(0)}\|e_{v}(\Gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.
``` |  | (28) |

Equation [28](#A6.E28 "In F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is a regularity condition relating the predictive test geometry to
conditional KL on the admissible law class; no universal reverse Pinsker
inequality is assumed.

###### Lemma F.1 (Finite-test coverage).

Suppose
Equation [28](#A6.E28 "In F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
holds and

|     |                                              |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       P_{v}=Q_{m,v}\Pi_{m,v}\psi_{v}(S_{v},C_{v}).
       ```                                           |     |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}(\Gamma)\leq c_{v}^{(0)}\Delta_{v}^{\rm test}(\Gamma)+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma),}
``` |  | (29) |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma):=c_{v}^{(0)}\|(I-\Pi_{m,v})e_{v}(\Gamma)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.
``` |  |

For a nested complete test family,
$`\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma)\to 0`$ pointwise in $`\Gamma`$.

###### Proof.

Orthogonality of $`\Pi_{m,v}`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|e_{v}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}=\|\Pi_{m,v}e_{v}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\|(I-\Pi_{m,v})e_{v}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.
``` |  |

Linearity of conditional expectation and the coordinate isometry imply

|     |                                        |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       m_{U,v}-m_{Z,v}=Q_{m,v}\Pi_{m,v}e_{v}.
       ```                                     |     |

Hence $`\Delta_{v}^{\rm test}=\|\Pi_{m,v}e_{v}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}`$.
Substitution into
Equation [28](#A6.E28 "In F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
yields
Equation [29](#A6.E29 "In Lemma F.1 (Finite-test coverage). ‣ F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

The domination condition is non-vacuous in standard discrete predictive
settings. For example, suppose $`S_{v}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}`$, take the
complete one-hot test $`\psi_{v}(S_{v})=e_{S_{v}}`$, and write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p_{U}(\cdot):=\mathbb{P}_{v}(S_{v}=\cdot\mid U_{v},C_{v}),\qquad p_{Z}(\cdot):=\mathbb{P}_{v}(S_{v}=\cdot\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v}).
``` |  |

If

|     |                                                       |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       p_{Z}(s)\geq\alpha_{v}>0\quad\text{a.s. for every }s,
       ```                                                    |     |

then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\delta_{v}(\Gamma)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{v}D_{\rm KL}(p_{U}\|p_{Z})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{v}\chi^{2}(p_{U},p_{Z})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{v}\sum_{s=1}^{K}\frac{(p_{U}(s)-p_{Z}(s))^{2}}{p_{Z}(s)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{v}}\mathbb{E}_{v}\|p_{U}-p_{Z}\|_{2}^{2}.`$ |  |

Thus
Equation [28](#A6.E28 "In F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
holds with $`c_{v}^{(0)}=\alpha_{v}^{-1}`$, and for the complete one-hot
family the test coverage residual is zero. More general finite sketches
need not determine the full conditional law; their uncovered component
is what $`\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}`$ records.

Thus $`\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}`$ is a representation/test
approximation term, not a sampling error.

#### F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value

The finite-test value in
Equation [27](#A6.E27 "In F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
uses the raw test coordinates, as in the main text. The profiled
population criterion additionally balances the target coordinates.
Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Sigma_{PP,v}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\overline{P}_{v}\overline{P}_{v}^{\top}],\qquad W_{P,v}:=(\Sigma_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I)^{-1/2},\qquad\widetilde{P}_{v}:=W_{P,v}\overline{P}_{v},
``` |  |

with $`\varepsilon>0`$, and let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{m}_{X,v}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\widetilde{P}_{v}\mid X_{v},C_{v}]=W_{P,v}m_{X,v}.
``` |  |

The corresponding balanced Bayes value gap is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{v}^{{\rm test},{\rm bal}}(\Gamma):=\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(\widetilde{P}_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C_{v})-\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(\widetilde{P}_{v}\mid U_{v},C_{v})=\mathbb{E}_{v}\|W_{P,v}(m_{U,v}-m_{Z,v})\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  |

Let $`s_{v}:=\sigma_{\min}(W_{P,v})>0`$. Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{v}^{\rm test}(\Gamma)\leq s_{v}^{-2}\Delta_{v}^{{\rm test},{\rm bal}}(\Gamma).
``` |  | (30) |

This step changes only the metric on the fixed finite test coordinates.

We next restrict the Bayes predictor to the fixed readout coordinates
used by the implementation and profile the readout exactly. For
notational simplicity, write $`X_{v}`$ for those fixed coordinates; if a
fixed lift is used, $`X_{v}`$ below denotes that lift. Define

|     |                                                          |     |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \overline{X}_{v}:=X_{v}-\mathbb{E}_{v}[X_{v}\mid C_{v}],
       ```                                                       |     |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Sigma_{XX,v}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\overline{X}_{v}\overline{X}_{v}^{\top}],\qquad\Sigma_{PX,v}:=\mathbb{E}_{v}[\overline{P}_{v}\overline{X}_{v}^{\top}],\qquad\Sigma_{XP,v}:=\Sigma_{PX,v}^{\top}.
``` |  |

For $`\lambda>0`$, consider

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{R}_{\lambda,\varepsilon,v}^{\rm lin}(X_{v}\mid C_{v}):=\min_{A}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{v}\|\widetilde{P}_{v}-A\overline{X}_{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|A\|_{F}^{2}\right\}.
``` |  | (31) |

###### Proposition 5 (Profiled predictive value).

The minimizer of
Equation [31](#A6.E31 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
A_{X,v}^{\star}=W_{P,v}\Sigma_{PX,v}(\Sigma_{XX,v}+\lambda I)^{-1},
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{R}_{\lambda,\varepsilon,v}^{\rm lin}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})={}&\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(\Sigma_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I)^{-1}\Sigma_{PP,v}\right]-J_{{\rm bal},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v}),\\
J_{{\rm bal},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v}):={}&\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(\Sigma_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I)^{-1}\Sigma_{PX,v}(\Sigma_{XX,v}+\lambda I)^{-1}\Sigma_{XP,v}\right].\end{aligned}}
``` |  | (32) |

Equivalently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J_{{\rm bal},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})=\left\|(\Sigma_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I)^{-1/2}\Sigma_{PX,v}(\Sigma_{XX,v}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Let $`G_{X,v}:=\Sigma_{XX,v}+\lambda I\succ 0`$. Expanding
Equation [31](#A6.E31 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and completing the square gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{v}\|\widetilde{P}_{v}-A\overline{X}_{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|A\|_{F}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}(W_{P,v}\Sigma_{PP,v}W_{P,v})-\operatorname{tr}\!\left(W_{P,v}\Sigma_{PX,v}G_{X,v}^{-1}\Sigma_{XP,v}W_{P,v}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\left\|(A-W_{P,v}\Sigma_{PX,v}G_{X,v}^{-1})G_{X,v}^{1/2}\right\|_{F}^{2}.`$ |  |

The final term is non-negative and vanishes at the stated minimizer.
Cyclicity of trace and $`\operatorname{tr}(BB^{\top})=\|B\|_{F}^{2}`$
yield the two forms of $`J_{{\rm bal},v}`$. ∎

The constant term in
Equation [32](#A6.E32 "In Proposition 5 (Profiled predictive value). ‣ F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is common to $`U_{v}`$ and $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}`$. Consistently with the
main text, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}(\Gamma):=J_{{\rm bal},v}(U_{v}\mid C_{v})-J_{{\rm bal},v}(Z_{v}^{\Gamma}\mid C_{v}).}
``` |  |

Profiling itself is exact within the ridge class. The approximation
restricts predictors to that class.

For $`X_{v}\in\{U_{v},Z_{v}^{\Gamma}\}`$ define its ridge oracle
residual

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle r_{{\rm lin},v}(X_{v})`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\lambda,\varepsilon,v}^{\rm lin}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})-\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\star}(\widetilde{P}_{v}\mid X_{v},C_{v})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\inf_{A}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{v}\|\widetilde{m}_{X,v}-A\overline{X}_{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|A\|_{F}^{2}\right\}\geq 0.`$ |  |

The second equality follows from the $`L^{2}`$ orthogonality of
$`\widetilde{P}_{v}-\widetilde{m}_{X,v}`$ to every square-integrable
function of $`(X_{v},C_{v})`$. Since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}=\Delta_{v}^{{\rm test},{\rm bal}}+r_{{\rm lin},v}(Z_{v}^{\Gamma})-r_{{\rm lin},v}(U_{v}),
``` |  |

we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\left|\Delta_{v}^{{\rm test},{\rm bal}}(\Gamma)-\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}(\Gamma)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma),}
``` |  | (33) |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma):=r_{{\rm lin},v}(U_{v})+r_{{\rm lin},v}(Z_{v}^{\Gamma}).
``` |  |

The term vanishes if both balanced conditional means lie in the readout
span as regularization vanishes.

Combining
Equations [29](#A6.E29 "In Lemma F.1 (Finite-test coverage). ‣ F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
[30](#A6.E30 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
and
[33](#A6.E33 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
gives the population profiled bridge

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{v}(\Gamma)\leq c_{v}\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}(\Gamma)+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma)+c_{v}\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma),\qquad c_{v}:=\frac{c_{v}^{(0)}}{s_{v}^{2}}.}
``` |  | (34) |

##### Width and the spectral oracle.

A width-$`r`$ transmitted state does not, by dimensionality alone,
induce a rank-$`r`$ orthogonal projection in the balanced covariance
geometry. A Ky–Fan rank-$`r`$ construction can still be used as a
diagnostic linear oracle when projection realizability is imposed, but
it is not needed for
Equation [34](#A6.E34 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and is not identified with the general nonlinear compressor. We
therefore do not use a Ky–Fan equality in the theoretical bridge from
$`\delta_{v}`$ to the implemented statistic.

#### F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency

We treat diagonal covariance as optional before deriving finite-sample
bounds for the full-covariance method.

##### Population diagonal geometry.

Define the stabilized diagonal marginal covariances

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{P,v}:=\operatorname{diag}(\Sigma_{PP,v})+\varepsilon I,\qquad D_{X,v}:=\operatorname{diag}(\Sigma_{XX,v})+\lambda I,
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J_{{\rm diag},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v}):=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[D_{P,v}^{-1}\Sigma_{PX,v}D_{X,v}^{-1}\Sigma_{XP,v}\right].
``` |  |

The corresponding population diagonal deficiency is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{v}^{\rm diag}(\Gamma):=J_{{\rm diag},v}(U_{v}\mid C_{v})-J_{{\rm diag},v}(Z_{v}^{\Gamma}\mid C_{v}).
``` |  |

Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
E_{P,v}:=\Sigma_{PP,v}-\operatorname{diag}(\Sigma_{PP,v}),\qquad E_{X,v}:=\Sigma_{XX,v}-\operatorname{diag}(\Sigma_{XX,v}),
``` |  |

and define the relative off-diagonal departures

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\rho_{P,v}:=\left\|D_{P,v}^{-1/2}E_{P,v}D_{P,v}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\rm op},\qquad\rho_{X,v}:=\left\|D_{X,v}^{-1/2}E_{X,v}D_{X,v}^{-1/2}\right\|_{\rm op}.
``` |  |

Here and below $`\|\cdot\|_{\rm op}`$ denotes spectral norm.

###### Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation).

For $`X_{v}\in\{U_{v},Z_{v}^{\Gamma}\}`$, suppose
$`\|\Sigma_{PX,v}\|_{F}\leq K_{X,v}`$. Then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|J_{{\rm bal},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})-J_{{\rm diag},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})\right|`$ |  | (35) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq K_{X,v}^{2}\left(\frac{\|E_{P,v}\|_{\rm op}}{\varepsilon^{2}\lambda}+\frac{\|E_{X,v}\|_{\rm op}}{\varepsilon\lambda^{2}}\right)=:\beta_{X,v}.`$ |  |  |

If additionally $`\rho_{P,v}<1`$ and $`\rho_{X,v}<1`$, the first display
can be replaced by the relative bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\beta_{X,v}^{\rm rel}:=\frac{K_{X,v}^{2}}{\varepsilon\lambda}\left[\frac{\rho_{P,v}}{(1-\rho_{P,v})(1-\rho_{X,v})}+\frac{\rho_{X,v}}{1-\rho_{X,v}}\right].
``` |  | (36) |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\left|\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}(\Gamma)-D_{v}^{\rm diag}(\Gamma)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{{\rm diag},v}(\Gamma),}
``` |  | (37) |

with $`\varepsilon_{{\rm diag},v}:=\beta_{U,v}+\beta_{Z,v}`$, or with
the corresponding relative bounds when their conditions hold.

###### Proof.

Abbreviate $`A:=\Sigma_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I`$, $`D_{P}:=D_{P,v}`$,
$`B:=\Sigma_{XX,v}+\lambda I`$, $`D_{X}:=D_{X,v}`$, and
$`C:=\Sigma_{PX,v}`$. The resolvent identity gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|A^{-1}-D_{P}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\frac{\|E_{P,v}\|_{\rm op}}{\varepsilon^{2}},\qquad\|B^{-1}-D_{X}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\frac{\|E_{X,v}\|_{\rm op}}{\lambda^{2}},
``` |  |

while
$`\|A^{-1}\|_{\rm op},\|D_{P}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\varepsilon^{-1}`$ and
$`\|B^{-1}\|_{\rm op},\|D_{X}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\lambda^{-1}`$. A
two-term telescope gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle A^{-1}CB^{-1}C^{\top}-D_{P}^{-1}CD_{X}^{-1}C^{\top}={}`$ | $`\displaystyle(A^{-1}-D_{P}^{-1})CB^{-1}C^{\top}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+D_{P}^{-1}C(B^{-1}-D_{X}^{-1})C^{\top}.`$ |  |

Using $`|\operatorname{tr}(MN)|\leq\|M\|_{\rm op}\|N\|_{\ast}`$ and
$`\|CBC^{\top}\|_{\ast}\leq\|C\|_{F}^{2}\|B\|_{\rm op}`$ proves
Equation [35](#A6.E35 "In Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

For the relative form, write $`A=D_{P}^{1/2}(I+R_{P})D_{P}^{1/2}`$ with
$`\|R_{P}\|_{\rm op}=\rho_{P,v}`$, and analogously for $`B`$. If
$`\rho<1`$, then $`\|(I+R)^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq(1-\rho)^{-1}`$ and
$`\|(I+R)^{-1}-I\|_{\rm op}\leq\rho/(1-\rho)`$. Substituting these two
bounds into the same telescope gives
Equation [36](#A6.E36 "In Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
Applying the single-state bound to $`U_{v}`$ and $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ and
using the triangle inequality proves
Equation [37](#A6.E37 "In Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

Equation [37](#A6.E37 "In Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
makes precise the sense in which diagonalization is a geometric
approximation. The error is controlled by the off-diagonal departure of
the population covariance geometry and is distinct from sampling noise.

##### Finite-sample moments.

Let $`\mathcal{B}`$ contain $`n_{\mathcal{B}}`$ independent sampling
units from the eligible-interface law. In the tree setting, a sampling
unit can be taken as one independent tree/trajectory cluster; for i.i.d.
interfaces it is one interface. Let hats denote the corresponding
empirical centered covariance blocks. The main empirical value uses full
regularized covariance blocks:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\widehat{J}_{{\rm bal},v,\mathcal{B}}(X_{v}):=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(\widehat{\Sigma}_{PP,v}+\varepsilon I)^{-1}\widehat{\Sigma}_{PX,v}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{XX,v}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widehat{\Sigma}_{XP,v}\right].}
``` |  | (38) |

The main empirical deficiency is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma):=\widehat{J}_{{\rm bal},v,\mathcal{B}}(U_{v})-\widehat{J}_{{\rm bal},v,\mathcal{B}}(Z_{v}^{\Gamma}).}
``` |  | (39) |

The $`U_{v}`$ term is a stop-gradient reference in optimization. The
diagonal variant replaces both regularized covariance inverses in
Equation [38](#A6.E38 "In Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
by their regularized diagonal inverses. Denote its value and gap by
$`\widehat{J}_{{\rm diag},v,\mathcal{B}}`$ and
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\rm diag}_{v,\mathcal{B}}`$.

###### Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation).

Fix $`\Gamma`$ independently of the sampling units in $`\mathcal{B}`$.
Suppose $`\varepsilon\geq\varepsilon_{0}>0`$,
$`\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}>0`$, and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\Sigma_{PX,v}\|_{F}\vee\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{PX,v}\|_{F}\leq K_{v},\qquad X\in\{U,Z\}.
``` |  |

On an event on which, simultaneously for $`X\in\{U,Z\}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{PP,v}-\Sigma_{PP,v}\|_{\rm op}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\omega_{PP,v},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{XX,v}-\Sigma_{XX,v}\|_{\rm op}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\omega_{XX,v},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{PX,v}-\Sigma_{PX,v}\|_{F}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\omega_{PX,v}^{F},`$ |  |

one has

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left|\widehat{J}_{{\rm bal},v,\mathcal{B}}(X_{v})-J_{{\rm bal},v}(X_{v}\mid C_{v})\right|\leq\Lambda_{X,v},
``` |  | (40) |

where

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Lambda_{X,v}:=\frac{K_{v}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\lambda_{0}}\omega_{PP,v}+\frac{K_{v}^{2}}{\varepsilon_{0}\lambda_{0}^{2}}\omega_{XX,v}+\frac{2K_{v}}{\varepsilon_{0}\lambda_{0}}\omega_{PX,v}^{F}.
``` |  |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma)-\Delta_{v}^{\rm bal}(\Gamma)\right|\leq\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v},\qquad\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v}:=\Lambda_{U,v}+\Lambda_{Z,v}.}
``` |  | (41) |

If the three displayed covariance-deviation bounds hold jointly with
probability at least $`1-\eta`$, then
Equation [41](#A6.E41 "In Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
holds with the same probability. Under standard bounded or sub-Gaussian
sampling assumptions, the deviation radii $`\omega_{PP,v}`$,
$`\omega_{XX,v}`$, and $`\omega_{PX,v}^{F}`$ admit the usual
dimension-dependent $`n_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1/2}`$ concentration, with
lower-order $`n_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}`$ terms as appropriate. For clustered
trees, $`n_{\mathcal{B}}`$ denotes the number of independent clusters
and the concentration constants depend on the corresponding cluster-norm
bound.

###### Proof.

The covariance bounds apply to the full regularized blocks. A four-term
telescope for the two inverse factors and the two cross-covariance
factors, together with the resolvent identity
$`\widehat{A}^{-1}-A^{-1}=\widehat{A}^{-1}(A-\widehat{A})A^{-1}`$,
yields
Equation [40](#A6.E40 "In Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
Applying it to $`U_{v}`$ and $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ gives
Equation [41](#A6.E41 "In Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
The probability statement follows whenever the covariance-deviation
event is supplied by an appropriate concentration bound, for example
standard bounded/sub-Gaussian covariance concentration or matrix
Bernstein for independent bounded cluster summaries. For the diagonal
variant, diagonal extraction is nonexpansive in spectral norm; the same
perturbation radius bounds its empirical value relative to
$`J_{{\rm diag},v}`$. ∎

When several fixed sketches are used, the statistic in
Equation [39](#A6.E39 "In Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is computed for each sketch and averaged before differentiation. The
same bounds apply sketchwise and combine by averaging (or by a union
bound when a simultaneous high-probability event is required).

###### Corollary F.1 (Controlled surrogate for the local predictive deficiency).

Under context localization, the test-domination condition, and the
regularity conditions of
Lemmas [F.1](#A6.Thmlemma1 "Lemma F.1 (Finite-test coverage). ‣ F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and
[F.3](#A6.Thmlemma3 "Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
suppose additionally that the covariance deviation event in
Lemma [F.3](#A6.Thmlemma3 "Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
holds with probability at least $`1-\eta`$. Then, with probability at
least $`1-\eta`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\begin{aligned} \delta_{v}(\Gamma)\leq{}&c_{v}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma)+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma)\\
&+c_{v}\Big(\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma)+\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v}\Big).\end{aligned}}
``` |  | (42) |

For the diagonal variant, if
Lemma [F.2](#A6.Thmlemma2 "Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
also holds, replace $`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}`$ by
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\rm diag}_{v,\mathcal{B}}`$ and add
$`c_{v}\varepsilon_{{\rm diag},v}`$ to the right-hand side. Absorbing
$`c_{v}`$ into the last two residual definitions for the main method
yields the compact form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\delta_{v}(\Gamma)\leq c_{v}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma)+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{{\rm lin},v}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{{\rm stat},v}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

Successively apply
Equations [29](#A6.E29 "In Lemma F.1 (Finite-test coverage). ‣ F.3.2 Finite Predictive Tests ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
[33](#A6.E33 "In F.3.3 Profiled Predictive Value ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
and
[41](#A6.E41 "In Lemma F.3 (Finite-sample perturbation). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
along the chain in
Equation [22](#A6.E22 "In F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
For the diagonal variant, insert
Equation [37](#A6.E37 "In Lemma F.2 (Diagonal covariance perturbation). ‣ Population diagonal geometry. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
before applying the same finite-sample perturbation estimate to the
diagonal empirical value. ∎

Corollary [F.1](#A6.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary F.1 (Controlled surrogate for the local predictive deficiency). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is the only bridge needed here from the ideal local criterion to the
implemented statistic. It does not assert that
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}}=\delta_{v}`$, nor that every
stochastic optimizer step monotonically decreases the Shannon defect.
Instead, it separates the discrepancy into identifiable terms that
shrink under richer tests, a better readout class, and more statistical
support, respectively. The optional diagonal variant also incurs the
covariance-geometry residual.

The preceding calibration statement is stratum-wise: it applies to any
fixed interface law for which the displayed moments and residuals are
defined. The implementation does not estimate a separate covariance
problem for every interface. Instead, aligned eligible interfaces are
pooled and the same moment functional is evaluated under the resulting
pooled empirical law. Because covariance inversion is nonlinear, we do
not identify this pooled statistic with a weighted average of the
stratum-wise statistics, nor with an independent estimator of every
$`\delta_{v}`$. Pooling is used to obtain a higher-support shared
statistic. The projection guarantee below applies directly to this
actual pooled statistic through its interface-wise differential.

#### F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection

Although $`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}}`$ is estimated jointly
from all eligible interfaces in the current window, its differential
decomposes through the individual compressed states. Enumerate them as
$`z_{1}^{\Gamma},\ldots,z_{n_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\Gamma}`$ and define, at
the current parameters $`\Gamma_{t}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{i}:=\nabla_{z_{i}^{\Gamma}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma_{t}),\qquad q_{i}:=\left(\nabla_{\Gamma}z_{i}^{\Gamma}\right)^{\top}a_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.
``` |  |

The adjoint $`a_{i}`$ is obtained from the jointly estimated statistic;
it is not the gradient of a separately estimated single-interface
covariance problem. The chain rule gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}}(\Gamma_{t})=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathcal{B}}}q_{i}.
``` |  |

Holding the other interface routes fixed, the first-order change
transmitted through interface $`i`$ under a parameter displacement $`d`$
is

|     |                             |     |      |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       q_{i}^{\top}d+o(\|d\|_{2}).
       ```                          |     | (43) |

Let $`t_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}`$ be the task proposal produced by the host
optimizer. For each group $`\mathcal{G}_{g}`$ used in the main-text
implementation, put $`\bar{q}_{g}:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}_{g}}q_{i}`$.
For $`\bar{q}_{g}\neq 0`$, require

|     |                                                                |     |      |
|-----|----------------------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \bar{q}_{g}^{\top}d\leq\kappa\|\bar{q}_{g}\|_{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}.
       ```                                                             |     | (44) |

Defining $`h_{g}:=\bar{q}_{g}/\|\bar{q}_{g}\|_{2}`$ gives
$`h_{g}^{\top}d\leq\kappa\|t_{t}\|_{2}`$. Groups with $`\bar{q}_{g}=0`$
impose no first-order restriction. Stack the remaining $`h_{g}^{\top}`$
as the rows of $`H_{t}`$, let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
b_{t}:=\kappa\|t_{t}\|_{2}\mathbf{1}_{m},\qquad\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\{d\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:H_{t}d\leq b_{t}\},
``` |  |

and define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
d_{t}^{\star}:=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{d\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}\frac{1}{2}\|d-t_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  | (45) |

This is the predictive feasibility projection used in
Proposition [2](#Thmprop2 "Proposition 2 (Dual Characterization of Predictive Feasibility Projection). ‣ 3.2 Margin-Relaxed Predictive Constraints ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and
Algorithm [2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.3 Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

Because $`\kappa\geq 0`$, $`0\in\mathcal{F}_{t}`$. The feasible set is
therefore a nonempty closed convex polyhedron, while the objective in
Equation [45](#A6.E45 "In F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is strictly convex and coercive. Hence $`d_{t}^{\star}`$ exists and is
unique. It is the constrained update closest to the task proposal, and
if $`t_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}`$ then $`d_{t}^{\star}=t_{t}`$.

For completeness, introduce multipliers $`\alpha\geq 0`$. Stationarity
of the Lagrangian gives

|     |                             |     |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       d=t_{t}-H_{t}^{\top}\alpha.
       ```                          |     |

The equivalent dual problem is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\alpha_{t}^{\star}\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{\alpha\geq 0}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|H_{t}^{\top}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+\alpha^{\top}(b_{t}-H_{t}t_{t})\right\},\qquad d_{t}^{\star}=t_{t}-H_{t}^{\top}\alpha_{t}^{\star}.}
``` |  | (46) |

The primal solution is unique even if the dual multiplier is not. The
KKT conditions show that the correction $`t_{t}-d_{t}^{\star}`$ is a
non-negative combination of active predictive directions.

##### Computational complexity.

Let $`p`$ be the number of trainable compressor parameters, $`m`$ the
number of grouped predictive constraints, and $`K`$ the number of
dual-solver iterations. Given $`H_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times p}`$, a
matrix-free dual solve uses products with $`H_{t}`$ and $`H_{t}^{\top}`$
and costs $`O(Kmp)`$ time and $`O(mp)`$ storage. If the Gram matrix
$`G_{t}=H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}`$ is cached, forming it costs $`O(m^{2}p)`$,
the dual iterations cost $`O(Km^{2})`$, and the final correction
$`H_{t}^{\top}\alpha_{t}^{\star}`$ costs $`O(mp)`$, with $`O(mp+m^{2})`$
storage. Thus the optimization is carried out in the $`m`$-dimensional
constraint space rather than as a $`p`$-dimensional QP; here $`m`$
counts active constraint groups and is much smaller than $`p`$.

The Euclidean projection theorem gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(t_{t}-d_{t}^{\star})^{\top}(d-d_{t}^{\star})\leq 0,\qquad\forall d\in\mathcal{F}_{t}.
``` |  |

Taking $`d=0`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
t_{t}^{\top}d_{t}^{\star}\geq\|d_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\|d_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}\leq\|t_{t}\|_{2}.
``` |  |

Thus the projection does not enlarge the proposed step. Moreover,
$`h_{g}^{\top}t_{t}\leq\|t_{t}\|_{2}`$ by Cauchy–Schwarz, so if
$`\kappa\geq 1`$ the task proposal is always feasible, whereas
$`\kappa=0`$ makes every retained group contribution non-increasing to
first order.

Finally, summing
Equation [43](#A6.E43 "In F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
within each group and applying primal feasibility gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\bar{q}_{g}^{\top}d_{t}^{\star}\leq\kappa\|\bar{q}_{g}\|_{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2},\qquad g=1,\ldots,m,}
``` |  |

and hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{g}(d_{t}^{\star})\leq\kappa\|\bar{q}_{g}\|_{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}+o(\|d_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}),
``` |  |

where $`\Delta_{g}(d)`$ denotes the combined change through group $`g`$.
Group feasibility does not imply the same bound for each
$`q_{i}^{\top}d_{t}^{\star}`$. This is intentionally a first-order
guarantee on the implemented predictive statistic. It is not a claim
that each finite stochastic update monotonically decreases the ideal
mutual-information defect.

##### Summary of the theoretical boundary.

The main text provides the ideal information-theoretic implication

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\text{local predictive deficiency}\;\Longrightarrow\;\text{root predictive stability}.
``` |  |

This appendix provides the separate full-covariance local calibration
bridge

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\text{local predictive deficiency}`$ | $`\displaystyle\longrightarrow\;\text{finite tests}\;\longrightarrow\;\text{profiled balanced value}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\;\longrightarrow\;\text{empirical statistic},`$ |  |  |

with explicit residuals $`\varepsilon_{\rm test}`$,
$`\varepsilon_{\rm lin}`$, and $`\varepsilon_{\rm stat}`$. The optional
diagonal variant adds $`\varepsilon_{\rm diag}`$. The calibration result
is stated for a fixed interface law.
Algorithm [2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.3 Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
uses the corresponding pooled moment functional and projects the task
proposal to control its interface-wise first-order variation. No step
requires the identification
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}}=\delta_{v}`$, and the pooled
statistic is not claimed to be an independent estimator of every local
predictive deficiency.

### F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization

This subsection gives the finite-horizon training statement associated
with the projected updates in
Section [3.3](#S3.SS3 "3.3 Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and connects that training-time control to
Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is pointwise in the compressor $`\Gamma`$: it converts small population
local predictive deficiencies into a small discrepancy at the root.
Proposition [2](#Thmprop2 "Proposition 2 (Dual Characterization of Predictive Feasibility Projection). ‣ 3.2 Margin-Relaxed Predictive Constraints ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
by contrast, controls the linearized change of the pooled empirical
statistic used by the optimizer at one training iterate. This appendix
connects the two statements over a finite optimization horizon. The
argument accounts for window refresh and pooled-to-local calibration.

At iteration $`t`$, write the optimization window as $`\mathcal{B}_{t}`$
and make the iteration dependence of the grouped sensitivities explicit
by writing $`\bar{q}_{g,t}`$. These are the same grouped directions as
$`\bar{q}_{g}`$ in
Appendix [F.3.5](#A6.SS3.SSS5 "F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
evaluated at $`(\Gamma_{t},\mathcal{B}_{t})`$. The host optimizer
proposes $`t_{t}`$, predictive feasibility projection returns
$`d_{t}^{\star}`$, and
Algorithm [2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.3 Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
sets $`\Gamma_{t+1}=\Gamma_{t}+d_{t}^{\star}`$.

###### Proposition 6 (Finite-horizon control of the pooled predictive statistic).

Suppose that, at every iterate $`t`$, the retained groups partition the
eligible interfaces entering
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}`$, and that
$`\Gamma\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma)`$ has an
$`L_{t}`$-Lipschitz gradient on the line segment joining $`\Gamma_{t}`$
and $`\Gamma_{t+1}`$. Then the projected update satisfies

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t+1})\leq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t})+\kappa\|t_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{g=1}^{m}\|\bar{q}_{g,t}\|_{2}+\frac{L_{t}}{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.`$ |  | (47) |

Consequently, for any $`T\geq 1`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})\leq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{0}}(\Gamma_{0})`$ |  | (48) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left[\kappa\|t_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{g=1}^{m}\|\bar{q}_{g,t}\|_{2}+\frac{L_{t}}{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t+1}}(\Gamma_{t+1})-\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t+1})\right|.`$ |  |  |

###### Proof.

For a fixed window $`\mathcal{B}_{t}`$, the chain-rule decomposition in
Appendix [F.3.5](#A6.SS3.SSS5 "F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t})=\sum_{g=1}^{m}\bar{q}_{g,t}.
``` |  |

By $`L_{t}`$-smoothness,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t+1})\leq\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t})+\sum_{g=1}^{m}\bar{q}_{g,t}^{\top}d_{t}^{\star}+\frac{L_{t}}{2}\|d_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  |

Primal feasibility gives
$`\bar{q}_{g,t}^{\top}d_{t}^{\star}\leq\kappa\|\bar{q}_{g,t}\|_{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}`$
for every retained group, while the projection property established in
Appendix [F.3.5](#A6.SS3.SSS5 "F.3.5 Interface Constraints and Projection ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
gives $`\|d_{t}^{\star}\|_{2}\leq\|t_{t}\|_{2}`$. Substitution proves
Equation [47](#A6.E47 "In Proposition 6 (Finite-horizon control of the pooled predictive statistic). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

When the optimization window is refreshed, add and subtract
$`\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t+1})`$ to obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t+1}}(\Gamma_{t+1})\leq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t})+\kappa\|t_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{g=1}^{m}\|\bar{q}_{g,t}\|_{2}+\frac{L_{t}}{2}\|t_{t}\|_{2}^{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t+1}}(\Gamma_{t+1})-\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{t}}(\Gamma_{t+1})\right|.`$ |  |

Summing this inequality from $`t=0`$ to $`T-1`$ gives
Equation [48](#A6.E48 "In Proposition 6 (Finite-horizon control of the pooled predictive statistic). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

Equation [48](#A6.E48 "In Proposition 6 (Finite-horizon control of the pooled predictive statistic). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is a finite-horizon drift bound, not a monotonicity statement. The first
term in each increment is the allowed first-order margin, the quadratic
term is the fixed-window linearization remainder, and the final sum
records the change caused by refreshing the optimization window. In
particular, the projection prevents an unconstrained accumulation of
first-order predictive drift only to the extent quantified by these
terms.

###### Corollary F.2 (Connection to root predictive stability).

Let $`\mathcal{B}_{T}^{\rm eval}`$ be an evaluation sample independent
of the training trajectory. Suppose the conditions of
Corollary [F.1](#A6.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary F.1 (Controlled surrogate for the local predictive deficiency). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
hold at $`\Gamma_{T}`$ for every compressed interface jointly on an
event of probability at least $`1-\eta`$. Then, on that event, for every
$`v\in V_{\mathrm{comp}}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\delta_{v}(\Gamma_{T})\leq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle c_{v}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})+c_{v}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}_{T}^{\rm eval}}(\Gamma_{T})-\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})\right|`$ |  | (49) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma_{T})+c_{v}\Big(\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma_{T})+\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v}\Big).`$ |  |  |

Consequently, under (M1)–(M3),

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\operatorname{TV}\!\left(\mathcal{L}(Y_{\rho}\mid U,C),\mathcal{L}(Y_{\rho}\mid Z^{\Gamma_{T}},C)\right)\right]`$ |  | (50) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\in V_{\mathrm{comp}}}\Bigg[c_{v}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})+c_{v}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{v,\mathcal{B}_{T}^{\rm eval}}(\Gamma_{T})-\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})\right|`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{{\rm test},v}(\Gamma_{T})+c_{v}\Big(\varepsilon_{{\rm lin},v}(\Gamma_{T})+\varepsilon_{{\rm stat},v}\Big)\Bigg]\Bigg\}^{1/2}.`$ |  |  |

Combining
Equation [50](#A6.E50 "In Corollary F.2 (Connection to root predictive stability). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
with
Equation [48](#A6.E48 "In Proposition 6 (Finite-horizon control of the pooled predictive statistic). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
gives an explicit finite-horizon connection from the projected training
updates to the population root stability bound.

###### Proof.

Apply
Corollary [F.1](#A6.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary F.1 (Controlled surrogate for the local predictive deficiency). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
on the independent evaluation sample $`\mathcal{B}_{T}^{\rm eval}`$ and
add and subtract
$`c_{v}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{B}_{T}}(\Gamma_{T})`$. Taking the
absolute value of the resulting pooled-to-local difference gives
Equation [49](#A6.E49 "In Corollary F.2 (Connection to root predictive stability). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
At uncompressed interfaces, $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma_{T}}=U_{v}`$ and the local
predictive deficiency is zero. Summing the local bounds and applying
Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
yields
Equation [50](#A6.E50 "In Corollary F.2 (Connection to root predictive stability). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

The additional pooled-to-local term in
Equation [49](#A6.E49 "In Corollary F.2 (Connection to root predictive stability). ‣ F.4 Finite-Horizon Control along Predictive Feasibility Optimization ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
is essential: the pooled training statistic is not identified with an
independent estimator of every $`\delta_{v}`$. Thus the dynamic bridge
requires no stronger identification than the one already used in
Appendix [F.3](#A6.SS3 "F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
It states precisely when the optimization control is informative for the
population root guarantee: the cumulative projected drift,
window-refresh discrepancy, local calibration residuals, and
pooled-to-local discrepancy must remain controlled.

### F.5 Proof of Root Predictive Stability

This subsection proves
Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
from
Section [3](#S3 "3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

This appendix proves
Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
under (M1)–(M3) of
Appendix [F.1](#A6.SS1 "F.1 Proof of Recursive Closure ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
In particular, the host state $`U_{v}`$ may depend on the incoming
compressed child tuple
$`R_{v}^{\Gamma}=Z_{\operatorname{ch}(v)}^{\Gamma}`$. We use the induced
joint law for a fixed measurable $`\Gamma`$ and condition throughout on
the full retained context $`C`$. All conditional kernels exist on
standard Borel state spaces.

###### Proof of Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").

Abbreviate $`Z_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ by $`Z_{v}`$, let
$`Y_{V}=(Y_{v})_{v\in V}`$, and set

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Gamma):=\sum_{v\neq\rho}I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},C).
``` |  |

An uncompressed interface has $`Z_{v}=U_{v}`$ and contributes zero. If
the sum is infinite, the claimed bound is immediate, so assume it is
finite.

1. Factorization conditional on the predictive tree and all compressed
states. The child-before-parent construction in (M1)–(M2) has
conditional joint kernel

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Pi_{\rho}(dy_{\rho}\mid c)\prod_{v\neq\rho}Q_{v}(dy_{v}\mid y_{\operatorname{pa}(v)},c)\prod_{v\neq\rho}\widetilde{K}_{v}(du_{v},dz_{v}\mid s_{v},r_{v},c),
``` |  |

where the products denote the finite child-before-parent kernel
composition and $`r_{v}=z_{\operatorname{ch}(v)}`$. For each local
kernel, write its disintegration with respect to the compressed
coordinate as

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{K}_{v}(du_{v},dz_{v}\mid s_{v},r_{v},c)=K_{v}^{\Gamma}(dz_{v}\mid s_{v},r_{v},c)\,L_{v}(du_{v}\mid s_{v},z_{v},r_{v},c),
``` |  |

where $`K_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ is the $`Z_{v}`$ marginal and $`L_{v}`$ is a
version of $`P_{U_{v}\mid S_{v},Z_{v},R_{v},C}`$. In the recursive
composition, every $`K_{v}^{\Gamma}`$ factor depends only on
$`(y,z,c)`$: its incoming state $`r_{v}`$ is a coordinate of the full
$`z`$ tuple. The remaining $`u`$ factors are the $`L_{v}`$ kernels and
have disjoint output coordinates. Disintegrating the joint law with
respect to $`(Y_{V},Z,C)`$ therefore gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
P_{U\mid Y_{V},Z,C}(du\mid y,z,c)=\bigotimes_{v\neq\rho}P_{U_{v}\mid S_{v},Z_{v},R_{v},C}(du_{v}\mid s_{v},z_{v},r_{v},c)\quad P_{Y_{V},Z,C}\text{-a.s.}
``` |  | (51) |

This conditions on the *full* compressed state tuple. It allows
$`U_{v}`$ to depend on its incoming child states and makes no
independence claim for the $`U_{v}`$’s given only $`(Y_{V},C)`$.

2. Compare with a reference that retains the incoming child states. For
fixed $`(z,c)`$, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
R_{U\mid Z,C}(du\mid z,c):=\bigotimes_{v\neq\rho}P_{U_{v}\mid Z_{v},R_{v},C}(du_{v}\mid z_{v},r_{v},c).
``` |  |

By
Equation [51](#A6.E51 "In Proof of Proposition . ‣ F.5 Proof of Root Predictive Stability ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and additivity of relative entropy for finite products,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(P_{U\mid Y_{V},Z,C}\,\middle\|\,R_{U\mid Z,C}\right)\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\neq\rho}I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},R_{v},C)`$ |  | (52) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=I(Y_{V};U\mid Z,C)+\mathbb{E}\!\left[D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(P_{U\mid Z,C}\,\middle\|\,R_{U\mid Z,C}\right)\right].`$ |  |  |

The second equality follows by inserting $`P_{U\mid Z,C}`$ into the
log-density ratio and averaging over $`Y_{V}`$ conditional on $`(Z,C)`$.
The finite-defect assumption below guarantees that this calculation has
no indeterminate $`\infty-\infty`$ term. In particular,
$`I(Y_{V};U\mid Z,C)`$ is at most the sum in the first line.

For each compressed interface, (M3) and the fact that $`Z_{v}`$ is a
function of $`U_{v}`$ give $`I(S_{v};R_{v}\mid U_{v},Z_{v},C)=0`$. The
chain rule in its two orders therefore gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},R_{v},C)`$ | $`\displaystyle=I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},C)-I(S_{v};R_{v}\mid Z_{v},C)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},C).`$ |  |

At an uncompressed node, the left and right sides are both zero without
using (M3). Combining these facts with
Equation [52](#A6.E52 "In Proof of Proposition . ‣ F.5 Proof of Root Predictive Stability ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
and the chain rule from $`Y_{V}`$ to its coordinate $`Y_{\rho}`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
I(Y_{\rho};U\mid Z^{\Gamma},C)\leq I(Y_{V};U\mid Z^{\Gamma},C)\leq\sum_{v\neq\rho}I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C).
``` |  | (53) |

3. Pass from conditional information to total variation. Because
$`Z^{\Gamma}`$ is a measurable function of $`U`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
I(Y_{\rho};U\mid Z^{\Gamma},C)=\mathbb{E}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(P_{Y_{\rho}\mid U,C}\,\middle\|\,P_{Y_{\rho}\mid Z^{\Gamma},C}\right).
``` |  |

Conditional Pinsker and Jensen’s inequality imply

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\operatorname{TV}\!\left(\mathcal{L}(Y_{\rho}\mid U,C),\mathcal{L}(Y_{\rho}\mid Z^{\Gamma},C)\right)\right]`$ |  | (54) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{1}{2}I(Y_{\rho};U\mid Z^{\Gamma},C)\right\}^{1/2}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\left\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\neq\rho}I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C)\right\}^{1/2}.`$ |  |  |

This is precisely
Proposition [3](#Thmprop3 "Proposition 3 (Root Predictive Stability). ‣ 3.4 Properties and Predictive Guarantees ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

##### Scope of the optimization statement.

The bound holds for each fixed $`\Gamma`$ under (M1)–(M3). The empirical
calibration in
Corollary [F.1](#A6.Thmcorollary1 "Corollary F.1 (Controlled surrogate for the local predictive deficiency). ‣ Finite-sample moments. ‣ F.3.4 Empirical Predictive Deficiency ‣ F.3 From Local Predictive Deficiency to Constrained Updates ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
additionally assumes test coverage, readout and covariance conditions,
and a suitable independent evaluation sample.
Proposition [2](#Thmprop2 "Proposition 2 (Dual Characterization of Predictive Feasibility Projection). ‣ 3.2 Margin-Relaxed Predictive Constraints ‣ 3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
controls the linearized change of the training statistic; by itself it
gives no bound on the value of that statistic at a later iterate,
particularly when the margin is positive or projection is not used.

### F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency

This subsection formalizes the limitation of endpoint-only supervision
discussed in
Section [3](#S3 "3 Learning for Predictive Preservation ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"):
matching the root-target posterior does not, in general, imply local
predictive sufficiency at recursive interfaces.

This appendix isolates the limited claim used in the main text:
root-target sufficiency does not, in general, imply local predictive
sufficiency for the adjacent predictive pair. We use the same
pre-compression state $`U_{v}`$ as in the main text and the preceding
appendices; no additional source state is introduced.

Fix a compressed node $`v`$ and suppress the subscript $`v`$ when no
confusion can arise. Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S:=S_{v}=(Y_{v},Y_{\operatorname{pa}(v)}),\qquad U:=U_{v},\qquad Z^{\Gamma}:=\Gamma(U),
``` |  |

and let $`T:=Y_{\rho}`$ denote the root target. Define the
probability-valued posterior states

|     |                                                            |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathsf{R}:=P_{S\mid U,C},\qquad\mathsf{Q}:=P_{T\mid U,C}.
       ```                                                         |     |

We assume that the root-target posterior is a measurable readout of the
local recursive posterior,

|     |                                                      |     |      |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathsf{Q}=\Lambda(\mathsf{R},C)\qquad P\text{-a.s.}
       ```                                                   |     | (55) |

for some measurable $`\Lambda`$. A sufficient condition is the
downstream Markov relation

|     |                                                                   |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       U\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow T\qquad\text{conditional on }C,
       ```                                                                |     |

because the root-target posterior is then obtained by integrating the
downstream task kernel against $`P_{S\mid U,C}`$.

For any representation $`Z`$ generated from $`(U,C)`$ without access to
$`(S,T)`$, define

|     |                                                                    |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \delta_{S}(Z):=I(S;U\mid Z,C),\qquad\delta_{T}(Z):=I(T;U\mid Z,C).
       ```                                                                 |     |

In particular,
$`\delta_{S}(Z^{\Gamma})=I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v}^{\Gamma},C)=\delta_{v}(\Gamma)`$.

###### Theorem F.1 (Task-posterior quotient).

Under
Equation [55](#A6.E55 "In F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),
define

|     |                                 |     |
|-----|---------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \Delta:=I(S;U\mid\mathsf{Q},C).
       ```                              |     |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\Delta=I(S;\mathsf{R}\mid\mathsf{Q},C)=\mathbb{E}\!\left[D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(\mathsf{R}\,\middle\|\,P_{S\mid\mathsf{Q},C}\right)\right],\qquad P_{S\mid\mathsf{Q},C}=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{R}\mid\mathsf{Q},C].}
``` |  | (56) |

Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\mathsf{R}\text{ is measurable with respect to }\sigma(\mathsf{Q},C)\quad P\text{-a.s.}
``` |  |

The root-target posterior itself is root-target sufficient but can fail
to be locally predictively sufficient:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{T}(\mathsf{Q})=0,\qquad\delta_{S}(\mathsf{Q})=\Delta.}
``` |  | (57) |

Hence, whenever $`\Delta>0`$, root-target sufficiency does not imply
recursive predictive sufficiency. In particular, under such a law there
is no distribution-free function $`g`$ with $`g(0)=0`$ satisfying

|     |                                    |     |      |
|-----|------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \delta_{S}(Z)\leq g(\delta_{T}(Z))
       ```                                 |     | (58) |

for every admissible representation $`Z`$.

###### Proof.

Because $`\mathsf{R}=P_{S\mid U,C}`$, the posterior state $`\mathsf{R}`$
is sufficient for $`S`$ relative to $`U`$:

|     |                             |     |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       S\perp U\mid(\mathsf{R},C).
       ```                          |     |

Likewise, because $`\mathsf{Q}=P_{T\mid U,C}`$,

|     |                             |     |
|-----|-----------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       T\perp U\mid(\mathsf{Q},C).
       ```                          |     |

Since $`\mathsf{R}`$ and $`\mathsf{Q}`$ are measurable functions of
$`(U,C)`$ and $`\mathsf{Q}`$ is measurable from $`(\mathsf{R},C)`$ under
Equation [55](#A6.E55 "In F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle"),

|     |                                                         |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       I(S;U\mid\mathsf{Q},C)=I(S;\mathsf{R}\mid\mathsf{Q},C).
       ```                                                      |     |

The conditional mutual-information representation then gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
I(S;\mathsf{R}\mid\mathsf{Q},C)=\mathbb{E}\!\left[D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(P_{S\mid\mathsf{R},\mathsf{Q},C}\,\middle\|\,P_{S\mid\mathsf{Q},C}\right)\right].
``` |  |

Because $`\mathsf{R}`$ is the posterior of $`S`$ given $`(U,C)`$ and
$`\mathsf{Q}`$ is a function of $`(\mathsf{R},C)`$,
$`P_{S\mid\mathsf{R},\mathsf{Q},C}=\mathsf{R}`$ almost surely. The tower
property gives
$`P_{S\mid\mathsf{Q},C}=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{R}\mid\mathsf{Q},C]`$,
proving
Equation [56](#A6.E56 "In Theorem F.1 (Task-posterior quotient). ‣ F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
The same identity shows that $`\Delta=0`$ exactly when
$`\mathsf{R}=P_{S\mid\mathsf{Q},C}`$ almost surely, equivalently when
$`\mathsf{R}`$ is $`\sigma(\mathsf{Q},C)`$-measurable.

Taking $`Z=\mathsf{Q}`$, posterior sufficiency gives

|     |                                                  |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \delta_{T}(\mathsf{Q})=I(T;U\mid\mathsf{Q},C)=0,
       ```                                               |     |

while by definition

|     |                                                       |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \delta_{S}(\mathsf{Q})=I(S;U\mid\mathsf{Q},C)=\Delta.
       ```                                                    |     |

Thus, if $`\Delta>0`$, $`Z=\mathsf{Q}`$ is an explicit representation
that is sufficient for the root target but not for the adjacent
predictive pair. Applying any proposed bound
$`\delta_{S}(Z)\leq g(\delta_{T}(Z))`$ with $`g(0)=0`$ to this
representation yields $`\Delta\leq 0`$, a contradiction. This proves
Equation [58](#A6.E58 "In Theorem F.1 (Task-posterior quotient). ‣ F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

##### An explicit compressor of the main-text form.

Let $`C`$ be constant, let $`A`$ and $`B`$ be independent fair bits, and
take $`U=(A,B)`$, $`S=(A,B)`$, and $`T=A`$. The one-bit map
$`\Gamma(U)=A`$ gives $`Z^{\Gamma}=\mathsf{Q}=A`$. Hence
$`I(T;U\mid Z^{\Gamma})=0`$ while $`I(S;U\mid Z^{\Gamma})=H(B)=\log 2`$
(in nats). This example establishes the claim within the class
$`Z^{\Gamma}=\Gamma(U)`$.

###### Corollary F.3 (One-way control under a downstream task channel).

If $`U\to S\to T`$ conditional on $`C`$, then for every admissible
representation $`Z`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\boxed{\delta_{S}(Z)=\delta_{T}(Z)+I(S;U\mid T,Z,C)\geq\delta_{T}(Z).}
``` |  | (59) |

Local predictive sufficiency implies root-target sufficiency, but
Theorem [F.1](#A6.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem F.1 (Task-posterior quotient). ‣ F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle")
gives a counterexample with $`\Delta>0`$.

###### Proof.

The downstream Markov relation and admissibility of $`Z`$ imply

|     |                       |     |
|-----|-----------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       T\perp(U,Z)\mid(S,C),
       ```                    |     |

and hence $`I(T;U\mid S,Z,C)=0`$. Applying the chain rule in the two
orders gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle I(S,T;U\mid Z,C)`$ | $`\displaystyle=I(S;U\mid Z,C)+I(T;U\mid S,Z,C)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\delta_{S}(Z),`$ |  |

and

|     |                                                   |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       I(S,T;U\mid Z,C)=I(T;U\mid Z,C)+I(S;U\mid T,Z,C).
       ```                                                |     |

Equating the two expressions proves
Equation [59](#A6.E59 "In Corollary F.3 (One-way control under a downstream task channel). ‣ An explicit compressor of the main-text form. ‣ F.6 Root-Target Supervision Does Not Imply Local Predictive Sufficiency ‣ Appendix F Theoretical Results and Proofs ‣ What to Preserve in Recursive Computation: A Local Predictive Sufficiency Principle").
∎

##### Relation to the main-text claim.

At node $`v`$, take $`S=S_{v}`$, $`U=U_{v}`$, and $`T=Y_{\rho}`$. When
the downstream task posterior collapses distinct local recursive
posteriors, $`\Delta_{v}>0`$. Then the representation
$`Z_{v}=\mathsf{Q}_{v}`$ is perfectly sufficient for the root target
while

|     |                                          |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       I(S_{v};U_{v}\mid Z_{v},C)=\Delta_{v}>0.
       ```                                       |     |

Therefore root-target supervision alone provides no distribution-free
reverse control of the local predictive deficiency. This is the only
conclusion from this appendix used by the main text; no minimum-rate
information bottleneck comparison and no additive joint-training
guarantee are required.
````
